## Codex host contract — measured (M6 additions to M5 H1–H17)

I measured every row below on 2026-09-26 against `PINNED_CODEX`.
- **Isolation.** Each probe used a scratch `git init` room, a scratch `CODEX_HOME`, a scratch `HOME`, and `env -i`.
- **Model.** A content-routed scripted Responses endpoint on `127.0.0.1`. It routes by a marker string in the request body, counts steps by `*_output` input items, and returns distinct usage per response.
- **Cost.** $0. `~/.codex` and `DEDICATED_CODEX_HOME` were never read or written.
- **Scripts:** `scratchpad/m6/{mock.mjs,drive.mjs,cap.sh,pathcap.sh}` and scripts `a,b,c,g,g2,g3,h,p`.

| # | Fact | How measured |
|---|---|---|
| X1 | **Tool surface of a catalog model.** The collaboration namespace is `spawn_agent`, `wait_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `interrupt_agent` and `list_agents`. `apply_patch` and `exec_command` are **nested inside the code-mode `exec` tool** (`code_mode_host` stable true). Requests carry `parallel_tool_calls: false`. | request `input[additional_tools]` |
| X2 | **`spawn_agent` is asynchronous.** Its output is `{"task_name":"/root/<name>"}`, and the child runs in the background. Parameters: `task_name`, `message`, `agent_type`, `fork_turns`, `model`, `reasoning_effort`. `fork_turns` **defaults to `all`**, so the child inherits the parent's whole history. `none` gives a fresh context. | tool schema; child `session_meta.forked_from_id` |
| X3 | **`wait_agent` is the serial primitive.** It blocks until a mailbox update from any live agent. It returns `{"message":"Wait completed.","timed_out":false}`. The child's final answer then arrives as an `agent_message`: `Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER … Sender: /root/<name> … Payload: <text>`. Timeout: default 30 000 ms, min 10 000, max 3 600 000. | probe C: parent spawn → wait → next request |
| X4 | **Code-mode nesting fires the same hooks.** A nested `tools.apply_patch(...)` fires PreToolUse/PostToolUse with `tool_name:"apply_patch"` and the M5-H8 `tool_input`. A nested `tools.exec_command({cmd})` fires `tool_name:"Bash"`, `tool_input.command`. The `exec` wrapper fires no hook. | probe B |
| X5 | **Child tool hooks are attributable.** Payloads from a subagent's tool calls carry `agent_id` and `agent_type`, the root `session_id`, and `cwd` = room. App-server notifications for child work carry `threadId` = the agent id. `hook/completed.params.run.id` ends with `:<call_id>`, and `subAgentActivity.id` equals the spawn `call_id`. | probe C |
| X6 | **SessionStart fires once per root thread's first turn and never for a subagent.** SubagentStart and SubagentStop fire exactly once per dispatch. | probe C capture |
| X7 | **Per-subagent usage exists on the host.** The SubagentStop `agent_transcript_path` rollout holds one `token_usage_record` per provider response, with `response_id`, `usage`, `turn_token_usage` and `thread_token_usage`. It equals the provider-reported usage exactly: `input_tokens` 102/103, `cached_input_tokens` 2/3 ← `input_tokens_details.cached_tokens`, `output_tokens` 12/13, thread total 230. `thread/tokenUsage/updated` fires per child thread. | probe C, mock-served log join on `response_id` |
| X8 | **`workspace-write` makes `.git` read-only.** `git worktree add` fails with `cannot lock ref … unable to create directory for .git/refs/heads/…`. `thread/start` `config: {sandbox_workspace_write:{writable_roots:["<room>/.git"]}}` fixes worktree add, commit, merge, worktree remove and branch -d. **Child agents inherit it.** Nothing is persisted to `CODEX_HOME`. | probes G, G3, H |
| X9 | **PATH differs between hooks and role shells.** Hooks are spawned by the `codex` process with the launcher PATH, prefixed by Codex-internal dirs, so `command -v cleargate` = the launcher's first. `exec_command` runs `/bin/zsh -lc`. `/etc/zprofile`'s `path_helper` puts `/usr/local/bin … /opt/homebrew/bin` **ahead** of the launcher PATH, so role shells resolve the **global** `/opt/homebrew/bin/cleargate` (0.26.1). A login zsh reads `$HOME/.zprofile` after `path_helper`. | probe P |
| X10 | A 62 KB `.agents/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md` is listed as a skill (`… (file: r1/sprint-execution/SKILL.md)`). | `codex debug prompt-input` |
| X11 | **TUI hook-trust flow** (binary strings). The startup notice is `' hooks need review before they can run.'`, with the choices `Review hooks`, `Trust all and continue` and `Continue without trusting (hooks won't run)`, and a `hooks_browser_view`. A `bypass_hook_trust` override exists; it is never used. | `strings` |
| X12 | **The embedded binary drifted.** `/Applications/ChatGPT.app/Contents/Resources/codex` is now `codex-cli 0.155.0-alpha.16.3`, sha256 `c67698d0990aae05211d9c43ab343ad9517e406824dea77eca103a2806232b3a`. It is no longer a same-version secondary target. | `--version`, `shasum` |

**Consequences, bound into the blueprints:**
- **Serial dispatch under Codex** = `spawn_agent` with `agent_type` and `fork_turns: "none"`, followed by `wait_agent` until that agent's `FINAL_ANSWER` arrives (X2, X3). It lives in the Codex rendering's host note, never in `dispatch-serial.md`.
- **The loop's git steps need a writable `.git`** (X8). -13 discloses the sandbox posture it uses, and files the product gap as a follow-up.
- **The clean room must fix role-shell PATH** (X9), or role commands test the published release (the M4 F1 class).
- **Metering.** The telemetry is verifiable (X7), but ClearGate does not consume it in SPRINT-41 (-12 §1.3; M5 AD-6). The ClearGate verdict is therefore `metering: unavailable`, and the probe result is recorded. The goal permits either "verified" or "explicitly unavailable"; M6 ships "explicitly unavailable" plus a measured follow-up.

## Architecture decisions (binding on both blueprints)

**AD-M6-1 — Dispatch mode is a static adapter field, read by `payloadFilter`.**

In `src/hosts/types.ts`:
```ts
export type DispatchMode = 'wave-parallel' | 'serial';
// HostAdapter gains:
/** Static, install-time: how this host's orchestrator dispatches a wave. 'wave-parallel' = Workflow
 *  primitive (launch_wave contract); 'serial' = no Workflow primitive (dispatch-serial.md). STORY-058-12. */
readonly dispatchMode: DispatchMode;
```

The adapters:
- `claudeAdapter`: `'wave-parallel'`
- `codexAdapter`: `'serial'`

`createHostRegistry` does **not** validate the field at runtime. The sealed -03 fakes lack it, and tests are not type-checked (M3 T4 item 5 precedent).

In `src/hosts/registry.ts`:
```ts
/** Install-relative root of the serial-dispatch sprint-execution surface. */
export const SERIAL_DISPATCH_SURFACE = '.agents/skills/sprint-execution';
```
Inside `payloadFilter`'s shared-root loop (`registry.ts:139-162` on `6b5ee5c`), a selected declarer admits a path under `SERIAL_DISPATCH_SURFACE` **only if** `registry.resolve(name).dispatchMode === 'serial'`. Every other shared path keeps today's rule. That is where "the installer reads `dispatchMode`" becomes true: a hypothetical `wave-parallel` host that shares `.agents` never receives the serial rendering.
- If `selectEntriesForHosts` does not delegate to `payloadFilter`, apply the same rule there **[verify after -07: grep `export function selectEntriesForHosts` in `src/hosts/manifest-query.ts` and whether it calls `payloadFilter`]**.

**AD-M6-2 — Single source, two renderings.**
- The canonical playbook stays `scaffold/hosts/claude/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/SKILL.md`. Its only change is P1/P2 below.
- `dispatch-serial.md` is authored once, at `scaffold/hosts/claude/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md`, as the story places it (OQ3: two files). It ships inert in Claude installs.
- The Codex copies are **generated** into `scaffold/core/.agents/skills/sprint-execution/{SKILL.md,dispatch-serial.md}` by `scripts/build-codex-payload.mjs --write`:
  - `SKILL.md` = `renderCodexSprintExecution(<canonical SKILL.md>)`
  - `dispatch-serial.md` = a byte-identical copy
- Prebuild runs `--check` (M5 AD-1), which fails the build on drift.
- `session-start.sh` is **unchanged**. Its `Load skill: sprint-execution` line (`:38-42`) is now satisfied under Codex through AGENTS.md's "open `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`" rule (M5).

**AD-M6-3 — The Codex rendering is five anchored replacements** (`scripts/codex-sprint-execution.mjs`). Anchors are exact line prefixes in the canonical file, each required exactly once.
- A missing anchor throws `codex-sprint-execution: anchor not found: <A#>`.
- A duplicate throws `… anchor not unique: <A#>`.
- After rendering, any surviving banned token throws `… banned token survived: <t>`.
- Line numbers are from `6b5ee5c` (for orientation only; the code keys on anchors):

| # | Anchor (range) | Canonical lines | Replacement |
|---|---|---|---|
| A5 | insert after the line `# Sprint Execution — The Playbook` | :15 | text R5 (host note), preceded and followed by one blank line |
| A1 | from the line starting `There is one and only one way a sprint executes` up to (excluding) `### 2.1 Spikes run before the loop, not inside it` | :104–:105, plus P1 after -12 | text R1, then one blank line |
| A2 | from `` ### C.0 Execution — run each wave via `/workflows` `` up to (excluding) `### C.1 Pre-execution check` | :265–:288, plus P2 after -12 | text R2, then one blank line |
| A3 | the single line starting `**Serial barrier merge.**` | :481 | text R3 |
| A4 | from the line starting ``The `pending-task-sentinel.sh` PreToolUse hook blocks the next`` through the line starting `**Between-wave relocation.**` (inclusive) | :578–:580 | text R4 |

- Banned tokens in the rendered output: `/workflows`, `Workflow tool`, `parallel()`, `launch_wave`, `resumeFromRunId`, `RUN_ID`, `mintRunId`, `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE=1`, `Between-wave relocation`.
- **Measured on `6b5ee5c`:** every occurrence of these tokens lies inside A1–A4 (lines 104, 265–287, 481, 578, 580).
- The frontmatter and every other line stay verbatim, in order.

**AD-M6-4 — The serial driver is a step-wise CLI over one pure core** (`scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs`, with a live twin).
- A model orchestrator cannot be driven from a node process, so the orchestrator calls `next` and `record`.
- `dispatchSerial()` (with seams) is the reference loop the unit tests drive. It mirrors `launchWave`'s seam shape (`launch_wave.mjs:264`).
- It imports `worktreeAddCommand`, `validateVerdicts` and `mintRunId` from `./launch_wave.mjs`, and `extractWorkItemIds` from `./lib/work-item-id.mjs`.
- It has no inline ID regex: `check-no-inline-id-regex.mjs:34-37` scans `scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts`.
- Its source never contains the identifier `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE`.

**AD-M6-5 — The flashcard gate is ported once to JS, pinned to the hook.**
- `scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts/lib/flashcard-gate.mjs` (plus its live twin) is an exact port of `pending-task-sentinel.sh`'s report glob (`:97-100`) and parser (`:110-159`).
- The port includes two bash quirks:
  - `read -r` **skips a final line with no trailing newline**;
  - `\r` is kept on CRLF lines, so quote-stripping then misses and the hash differs.
- A parity test runs the real hook and the JS function over the same fixtures (the BUG-041 "shared corpus" pattern). This is the one sanctioned duplicate: scaffold scripts cannot import the hook's logic.

**AD-M6-6 — The verdict opt-in is a mode, not a second validator.** The signature becomes `validateVerdicts(verdicts, options?)`, with `options.mode ∈ {'wave','serial'}`, default `'wave'`.
- **Unknown mode:** throws `TypeError("validateVerdicts: unknown mode '<m>'")`.
- **`'wave'` / absent:** behavior is byte-for-byte today's (`launch_wave.mjs:129-224`).
- **`'serial'`**, in addition:
  - `tokens` may be exactly `{ metering: 'unavailable' }`: an object whose only own key is `metering`, with that value. Any other `metering` value, or any extra key such as a synthesized `input: 0`, is rejected: `'tokens' must be the measured object or exactly { metering: 'unavailable' }`.
  - A non-GREEN verdict may carry `devSha: null` and/or `qaSha: null`. An absent key is still rejected: `'devSha' must be a non-empty string, or null on a non-GREEN serial verdict`.
  - GREEN still requires both SHAs.
- A story blocked at QA-Red has no dev commit. Requiring a SHA would force invented evidence (`plans/codex-bootstrap-review.md` §5 observed the same defect).

**AD-M6-7 — -13's clean room is a synthetic user project, never this repository.**
- **Location.** A sibling container `~/Documents/Dev/cg-cleanroom-<UTC>-codexloop/`, validated by -09's `validateContainer`, holding:
  - `repo-loop/`: fresh `git init -b main`, seeded, `cleargate init --yes --host codex` from the packed artifact;
  - `home/`: holds `.zprofile`, `.zshrc` and `.bash_profile`, each exactly `export PATH="<C>/tools/bin:$PATH"` (X9);
  - `codex-home/`: scripted runs only;
  - `evidence/`.
- **The real story** is STORY-001-01, "slugify helper" (fixture below). It is authored with the artifact's `cleargate new` exactly as a drafting agent does, stripping the `<instructions>` block (protocol "Guidance Surface Reach": "Authored instances carry zero `<instructions>` blocks"). Its sprint is SPRINT-01, started with `cleargate sprint init`. Authored items pass `assert_story_files.mjs:162`, so BUG-090 does not arise. -09's `sprint-init` known-fail (un-authored path) is unaffected.
- **Isolation assertion.** The Codex process and every child get the scrubbed env (-08 doc §2) plus `HOME=<C>/home`, `CODEX_HOME`, and `PATH=<C>/tools/bin:…`. The gating check `codex.loop.meta-repo-untouched` asserts that no line naming `STORY-001-01` or `SPRINT-01` was appended during the run window to:
  - `OUTER/.cleargate/sprint-runs/*/token-ledger.jsonl`
  - `OUTER/.cleargate/hook-log/*.log`
  - `OUTER/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-41/{state.json,events.jsonl}`

**AD-M6-8 — Evidence modes and trust posture for -13.**

| Mode | Trust | Role | Gating |
|---|---|---|---|
| `provider: live` (`DEDICATED_CODEX_HOME`, real model `gpt-5.6-terra`) | `human` | **primary** (story OQ1/OQ3) | yes |
| `provider: scripted` (content-routed mock, `<C>/codex-home`) | `harness-exact-hash` | deterministic regression witness, labelled | yes |

- **Human posture.** `prepare` builds the room, then the owner opens the Codex TUI in it and trusts the workspace and the exact hooks (X11). `run --trust human` then:
  - refuses (exit 2) unless every project hook is `trusted` with `trusted_hash == currentHash`;
  - makes **zero** `config/batchWrite` calls (counted by the client wrapper);
  - asserts that the `hooks.state.*` and `projects."<room>"` subtrees of `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml` are unchanged from before the run.
- `--dangerously-bypass-hook-trust` / `bypass_hook_trust` is never used.
- The embedded binary is **not run** (X12). Its identity is recorded as drift.

**AD-M6-9 — Session posture for every -13 session:**
```
thread/start {
  cwd: <room>,
  model: <slug>,
  approvalPolicy: 'never',
  sandbox: 'workspace-write',
  config: { sandbox_workspace_write: { writable_roots: ['<roomRealpath>/.git'] } }
}
```
- The evidence records it verbatim as `sandbox_posture`.
- Server requests (for example `request_user_input` approvals) are answered `decline` and recorded (M5 `runTurn`).

## Per-story blueprint

### STORY-058-12 — host dispatch-mode split and serial fallback

**Goal tie:** -12 gives Codex an execution path without touching Claude's. Without it, condition 5 has no loop to prove. The Codex payload of M5 would load a skill that tells the orchestrator to halt.

**Coupling:**
- -03 (`types.ts:9` names this story), -04 (`payloadFilter`), -06 (manifest owners) — merged.
- -07 `selectEntriesForHosts` **[verify after -07]**.
- -10: `codexAdapter`, `build-codex-payload.mjs`, `openInstallView`, `composeCanonicalView`, harness. **[verify after -10]**
- -11:
  - `PORTABLE_SKILLS` generator, `.agents/skills/**` `TIER_RULES`, `SANCTIONED_BASELINE_ADDITIONS`;
  - enforcement §16;
  - `codex.live.skills` and its tests, portable-skills scenario 6.
  **[verify after -11]**
- -13 drives `dispatch_serial.mjs`, the rendering and serial verdicts.

**Schema changes:** none in any database. `HostAdapter.dispatchMode` (AD-M6-1). Verdict serial mode (AD-M6-6).

#### Files — cli (`story/STORY-058-12`)

**Pass A — dispatch contract:**

- **`scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts/launch_wave.mjs`:** the AD-M6-6 mode.
  - Keep every existing export and message.
  - Update the header comment's schema block with the serial deltas.
- **Create `scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts/lib/flashcard-gate.mjs`** (AD-M6-5):
  ```js
  export function cardHash(card)                  // sha1 of the UTF-8 card, first 12 hex (== printf '%s' | shasum -a 1 | cut -c1-12)
  export function flaggedCards(reportText)        // exact port of pending-task-sentinel.sh:110-159 (yaml inline [] → none; yaml block; "## flashcards_flagged" md block;
                                                  //   stop rules; quote strip " then '; empty skipped; split on "\n" only; a final segment without "\n" is ignored)
  export function reportFiles(sprintDir)          // regular files matching <sprintDir>/*-dev.md, *-qa.md, reports/*-dev.md, reports/*-qa.md (one level)
  export function pendingFlashcards({ sprintDir }) // → { ok: boolean, unprocessed: Array<{ card, hash, report }> } deduped by hash; ok iff none lacks <sprintDir>/.processed-<hash>
  ```
- **Create `scaffold/core/.cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs`** (AD-M6-4):
  ```js
  export const SERIAL_EXIT = Object.freeze({ OK: 0, INVALID: 1, USAGE: 2, HALT: 3, FLASHCARDS: 4 });
  export function sprintBranchFor(sprintId)        // 'SPRINT-01' → 'sprint/S-01'; throws unless /^SPRINT-\d{2,3}$/ (SPRINT-only pattern — exempt from the inline-id gate)
  export function serialLogPath(repoRoot, sprintId, waveId) // <repo>/.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint>/serial/<wave>.verdicts.jsonl
  export function readVerdictLog(file)             // [] when absent; one JSON object per line
  export function nextAction({ wave, verdicts, resume = false })
    // latest verdict per storyId (append order); walk wave.stories in order:
    //   GREEN → skip; non-GREEN → resume ? {action:'dispatch', storyId, resumed:true} : {action:'halt', storyId, verdict};
    //   none → {action:'dispatch', storyId, resumed:false}; all GREEN → {action:'wave-complete'}
  export async function dispatchSerial({ sprintId, sprintBranch, wave, verdicts = [], resume = false,
                                         dispatchStory, createWorktree, worktreeExists, flashcardGate, recordVerdict, env = process.env })
    // no dispatchStory → { status:'planned', steps:[{ storyId, worktreeCommand }] }, no seam called, env untouched
    // loop: a = nextAction(...) (resume applies to the first re-dispatch only)
    //   wave-complete → return; halt → return {status:'halted', ...}
    //   g = await flashcardGate({ sprintId, storyId }); !g.ok → return {status:'flashcards-pending', storyId, unprocessed}
    //   cmd = worktreeAddCommand(storyId, sprintBranch); if (!(await worktreeExists(storyId))) await createWorktree(cmd, storyId)
    //   v = await dispatchStory(storyId); validateVerdicts([v], { mode: 'serial' })  // throws naming storyId; nothing recorded
    //   await recordVerdict(v); verdicts.push(v); non-GREEN → return {status:'halted', storyId, verdict:v, dispatched, verdicts}
  ```
  - Every story id is checked with `extractWorkItemIds(id)` deep-equal `[id]` before any use (shell safety).
  - The default seams pass `env` to child processes and never read or write the flashcard-skip key.
  - **CLI**, run as `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs <cmd>`:
    - **Main guard:** `realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url)) === realpathSync(process.argv[1])`. The `file://` string form of `launch_wave.mjs:309` breaks under `/var` → `/private/var`.
    - **Repo root:** `--repo <dir>`, else `ORCHESTRATOR_PROJECT_DIR`, else `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` of cwd.

    | Command | Output (one JSON line on stdout) | Exit |
    |---|---|---|
    | `next --sprint <SPRINT-ID> --wave <wave-id> [--resume]` | `{"action":"dispatch","storyId","runId","worktreeCommand","worktreeExists","resumed"}` — `runId` = `mintRunId`; `worktreeExists` from `git worktree list --porcelain` | 0 |
    | ″ | `{"action":"process-flashcards","storyId","unprocessed":[{card,hash,report}]}` — gate runs before every `dispatch` answer | 4 |
    | ″ | `{"action":"halt","storyId","verdict"}` | 3 |
    | ″ | `{"action":"wave-complete"}` | 0 |
    | `record --sprint … --wave … --verdict <file\|->` | validates `mode:'serial'`, requires `storyId ∈ wave.stories`, appends `{...verdict, recorded_at}`; prints `{"recorded":true,"halt":<bool>}` | 0 GREEN · 3 non-GREEN · 1 invalid (stderr names the storyId; nothing appended) |
    | `--help` | usage | 0 |
    | anything else, missing flag, missing `plans/waves.json`/wave | usage error on stderr | 2 |

  - `record` never opens `token-ledger.jsonl`.

Claude Code declares `dispatchMode: wave-parallel`, so on Claude Code the halt above stands.

Unit `test/scripts/dispatch-serial.red.node.test.ts` (`dispatchSerial` with recording seams; valid ids `STORY-001-01..03`, `sprint/S-99`). This covers Gherkin 3–5:

5. **Strict ordering.**
   - The event log is `gate(1) wt(1) dispatch(1) record(1) gate(2) wt(2) …`.
   - Story 2's `dispatchStory` is not entered until story 1's promise has resolved and `recordVerdict(1)` has returned. Use deferred promises.
6. **Byte-identical worktree command.**
   - Every `createWorktree` receives `worktreeAddCommand(id,'sprint/S-99')` from the canonical `launch_wave.mjs`.
   - It also equals the literal `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-001-01 -b story/STORY-001-01 sprint/S-99`.
7. **Halt (Gherkin 4).**
   - Story 2 returns BLOCKED → `status:'halted'`, `storyId` STORY-001-02.
   - Dispatches are `[1,2]` and gates `[1,2]`.
   - `createWorktree` and `flashcardGate` are never called for story 3.
   - The function resolves right after `recordVerdict(2)` returns.
8. **Resume.**
   - Input: `verdicts=[1 GREEN, 2 BLOCKED]`, `resume:true`, and `worktreeExists(2)=true`. Stories 2 and 3 return GREEN.
   - Expected: dispatches `[2,3]`; story 1 never; no `createWorktree` for 2; `createWorktree` for 3.
   - The same input with `resume:false` → `halted` with zero seam calls.
   - With `resume:true` and story 2 BLOCKED again → dispatches `[2]` and halted; story 3 never.
9. **Env hygiene (Gherkin 5).**
   - `env` is a recording `Proxy` over `{}`. Across a 3-GREEN run and a halted run, no trap (`get`, `set`, `has`, `deleteProperty`, `defineProperty`) sees the key `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE`.
   - `process.env.SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE` is identical before and after, captured inside each seam.
   - The source text of the canonical `dispatch_serial.mjs` does not contain `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE`.
10. **Flashcards pending:** `flashcardGate(2)` returns `{ok:false}` → `status:'flashcards-pending'`, and there is no worktree and no dispatch for 2.
11. **Plan-only:** no `dispatchStory` → `status:'planned'` with 3 steps, and no seam call.
12. **Malformed verdict:** it rejects with `/Malformed segment verdict for STORY-001-02/`, and `recordVerdict` is not called for 2.

| `codex.loop.outcome` | At `sprint/S-01`, `git ls-tree` has `src/slugify.mjs`. The import probe returns `hello-world`, and `npm test` exits 0. |
| `codex.loop.serial-verdict` | `SPRINT-01/serial/wave1.verdicts.jsonl` holds a GREEN line for STORY-001-01 that validates in `mode:'serial'`, with `tokens` deep-equal to `{metering:'unavailable'}`. |
| `codex.live.discovery`, `session-start`, `post-tool-use`, `edit-deny`, `edit-allow`, `dispatch-deny`, `dispatch-identity` | -10 definitions (M5 verdict table) over S1–S4. `edit-deny` is INCONCLUSIVE, not FAIL, when a live attempt made no apply_patch call (for example it used the shell); the file must still be absent. |
| `codex.loop.hook-matrix` | **Every** `apply_patch` item has a completed or blocked PreToolUse run and a PostToolUse run joined by `call_id` (X5). **Every** spawn has a dispatch-gate PreToolUse run. **Every** dispatch has a completed SubagentStop run. SessionStart completed exactly once per session root thread. |
| `codex.loop.no-synthetic-usage` | The room's `token-ledger.jsonl` is absent or empty at the end of the run. |
| `codex.loop.path-identity` | The resolved cleargate is `<C>/tools/bin/cleargate` in **both** places: the harness's hook-side probe (`bash -c 'command -v cleargate'` under the Codex env), and a scripted/live `exec_command` `command -v cleargate` recorded in S2. |
| `codex.loop.meta-repo-untouched` | AD-M6-7 |
| `codex.loop.metering` (INFO) | `unavailable`. Per dispatch: rollout found, `token_usage_record` count, totals, and in scripted mode `matchesProvider` (join on `response_id` to `served`). |
| `codex.loop.trust-posture` (INFO; `check` enforces) | `human` \| `harness-exact-hash`, plus the (key, currentHash, trusted_hash) list and `configWrites`. |
| `codex.loop.wall-clock` (INFO) | ms per dispatch and per session |

- `loop_transport` = PASS iff all `LOOP_TRANSPORT_IDS` PASS.
- `enforcement_parity` = FAIL if any `ENFORCEMENT_IDS` FAILs; else INCONCLUSIVE if any is INCONCLUSIVE; else PASS.
- `result` = PASS iff both are PASS **and** `verify-install` and `doctor` exited 0.

**Per-dispatch hook matrix** (a rendered table; one row per root session and per dispatch). The columns are:
- `#`, `role`, `agent_id`
- `SessionStart`: root = `completed ×1`; dispatch = `not fired (host: root session only, X6)`
- `PreToolUse edit (n/N)`
- `PostToolUse (n/N; ingest lines)`
- `dispatch gate`: `completed|blocked (<call_id>)`
- `SubagentStop`
- `codex-identity`
- `wall-clock`

A hook that had no trigger reads `n/a (no <trigger> by this role)`. A trigger without its hook is a **gap** and FAILs `hook-matrix`.

**Evidence format** (`codex-loop-evidence.md`; the parser anchors are marked `†`):
```
# Codex loop evidence — SPRINT-41 (STORY-058-13)

Procedure: `.cleargate/knowledge/clean-room-verification.md` § Other hosts → Codex · Gate: STORY-058-13 `deferred_verification`
(close_sprint.mjs Step 2.9) — the LAST scripted run and the LAST live human-trust run must both be PASS against the final cli artifact inputs.

## Run <n> — <ISO UTC> — <label>                                  †
- result: PASS|FAIL                                                 †
- provider: scripted|live                                           †
- trust_posture: human|harness-exact-hash                           †
- codex_sha256: <64 hex>                                            †
- artifact_inputs: <64 hex>                                         †
- loop_transport: PASS|FAIL
- enforcement_parity: PASS|FAIL|INCONCLUSIVE
- metering: unavailable
- cli_sha · artifact tgz sha256 · container · codex_home · home
- codex: <abs path> · <--version> · origin <URL> (archive sha256 <hex>)
- model · sandbox_posture (AD-M6-9 verbatim) · approvals: never
- cleargate resolved: hooks <path> · role shells <path>
- merge_sha: <40 hex>
- embedded codex (not run): <path> · <version> · sha256 <hex>

### Controls           (S1–S4: id | attempt | result | evidence pointer)
### Loop checks        (the LOOP_TRANSPORT_IDS rows)
### Per-dispatch hooks (matrix)
### Metering           (per dispatch: rollout, records, totals, provider match)
### Trust              (posture, hooks list, configWrites)
### State transitions  (from events.jsonl)
### Gaps               (recorded, never omitted — each with its follow-up id)
### Goal Acceptance Check mapping (condition 5 → loop_transport + enforcement_parity + metering line)
```

`checkLoopEvidence` passes iff:
- the **last** `provider: scripted` run with `codex_sha256 == codexSha256` is PASS, with `artifact_inputs` equal to `computeArtifactInputs(<cli>, <ref>)`; **and**
- the **last** `provider: live` + `trust_posture: human` run with the same sha is PASS, with the same `artifact_inputs`.

Its reasons are `no scripted run`, `last scripted run FAIL`, `needs a live human-trust PASS run on the final artifact inputs`, and `stale`.

#### Files — outer (`.worktrees/STORY-058-13`)

- Create `.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-41/codex-loop-evidence.md`:
  - Run 1 (scripted, pass B);
  - Run 2 (live, human; step L, committed by the orchestrator on `story/STORY-058-13`).
- `.cleargate/knowledge/clean-room-verification.md`: under the pinned `## Other hosts` heading, replace the one-line pointer with `### Codex (STORY-058-13)`. It covers:
  - prepare → owner trust → run;
  - `CODEX_HOME` + `HOME` + the `.zprofile` PATH fix (X9);
  - the sandbox posture (X8);
  - the scripted vs live vs human-trust semantics;
  - `check`.

  The -08 doc test's pinned headings stay.
- Orchestration rows.

#### How Dev and QA run

- `npm run typecheck`.
- The new files, one at a time.
- -08/-10 verify-install files and every `test/commands/{doctor,upgrade,uninstall,host}*` file.
- The -10/-11/-12 harness unit files.
- The rule-16 `CLEARGATE_META_ROOT` sweep with `…/.worktrees/STORY-058-13`, because the doc edit is outer.
- The -09 e2e and the -10 codex e2e on the committed branch.
- `CLEARGATE_CODEX_BIN=<PINNED_CODEX> npm run test:file -- test/e2e/codex-loop.red.integration.node.test.ts`. Commit first.
- M2 parity 5/5 and 15/15. -13 changes no installed byte.

**Pass split:**
- **Pass A** makes green: 1–13 and 24.
- **Pass B** makes green: 14–23. It then runs:
  ```
  node scripts/clean-room/codex-loop.mjs run --codex-bin <PINNED_CODEX> --provider scripted --trust harness --ref story/STORY-058-13 --evidence OUTER/.worktrees/STORY-058-13/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-41/codex-loop-evidence.md --label "wave17 scripted"
  ```
  The result must be PASS. That is Run 1; commit it on the outer story branch.
- Commits:
  - `feat(EPIC-058): STORY-058-13 pass A — verify-install for Codex`;
  - `feat(EPIC-058): STORY-058-13 pass B — Codex loop harness and evidence`.

#### QA-Verify for -13

- Everything above, on the committed branch.
- **Reproducibility.** A `run --provider scripted --trust harness --ref story/STORY-058-13` into a fresh sibling container, **without** `--evidence`. Its `summary.json` verdict ids and statuses must equal Run 1's, INFO rows excepted.
- Record both container paths in `reports/STORY-058-13-qa.md`. Leave the containers in place.

#### Step L — the primary live run (orchestrator plus owner, after post-flight PASS, before DevOps)

1. **T-M6-8.** Pinned version and sha. `CODEX_HOME=<DEDICATED_CODEX_HOME> <PINNED_CODEX> login status` must exit 0; that is the owner's M5 OD1. The outer and cli story branches are committed and clean.
2. Prepare the room:
   ```
   node cleargate-cli/scripts/clean-room/codex-loop.mjs prepare --codex-bin <PINNED_CODEX> --provider live --codex-home <DEDICATED_CODEX_HOME> --ref story/STORY-058-13
   ```
3. **Owner action (OD-M6-1).** Run the printed command:
   ```
   cd <room> && env -i PATH="<C>/tools/bin:/usr/bin:/bin:/usr/sbin:/sbin" HOME="<C>/home" CODEX_HOME="<DEDICATED_CODEX_HOME>" TERM="$TERM" <PINNED_CODEX>
   ```
   - Trust the workspace when asked.
   - At "N hooks need review before they can run", choose **Review hooks**, read the five ClearGate commands, and trust them. If the notice does not appear, open the hooks browser (`/hooks`) and trust each ClearGate hook.
   - Quit without sending a prompt, so that no turn and no model call happens.
4. Run the primary:
   ```
   node cleargate-cli/scripts/clean-room/codex-loop.mjs run --container <C> --trust human --model gpt-5.6-terra --evidence OUTER/.worktrees/STORY-058-13/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-41/codex-loop-evidence.md --label "wave17 primary"
   ```
   It must be PASS. That is Run 2.
   - **Exit 2 (trust missing):** redo step 3.
   - **FAIL:** allow one more attempt with a fresh `prepare` (a new container, so trust is granted again).
   - **A second FAIL:** -13 is BLOCKED, with `blocker.type` `technical-impossibility` or `spec-contradiction` as the evidence shows. File the gaps and halt for the owner. No waiver (story OQ4).
5. Commit the evidence on `story/STORY-058-13` in `.worktrees/STORY-058-13`:
   ```
   evidence(STORY-058-13): primary live human-trust Codex loop run
   ```
   The surface gate passes, because the path is a §3.1 row.
6. **File every gap.** For each `### Gaps` row that has no item yet, draft a Draft BUG or CR with `node OUTER/cleargate-cli/dist/cli.js new <type> "<slug>"` (rule 18). Put the IDs in the evidence `### Gaps` column, amend Run 2's section, and commit. The known gaps, citing M5 follow-ups rather than duplicating them:
   - **Sandbox:** `.git` read-only under Codex `workspace-write` (X8). CR: declare the `.git` writable root for the ClearGate loop in the Codex install, or document it.
   - **Metering consumer:** read the SubagentStop `agent_transcript_path` `token_usage_record`s (X7). CR.
   - **Role TOMLs** carry Claude-host paths (`.claude/…`) in `developer_instructions` (-11 codegen). CR.
   - **Autonomy guard:** there is no `request_user_input` autonomy guard on Codex (M5 follow-up).
   - **Git hooks:** there are no git pre-commit hooks for codex-only repos (M5 follow-up).
   - **INFO, no item:** SessionStart is root-only (X6), by host design.

#### DevOps (two-repo, one turn, rule 15)

1. **Outer half:** the evidence (Runs 1–2 and any Gaps amend), the clean-room doc, the story, reports, `wiki/log.md`.
2. **Cli half.** Leave the checkout on `main`.
3. **Full suite**, then BD.
4. **Post-BD:**
   - `node cleargate-cli/scripts/clean-room/codex-loop.mjs check --evidence OUTER/.cleargate/sprint-runs/SPRINT-41/codex-loop-evidence.md` exits 0. The story-branch trees equal `main`'s for every artifact-input path under R1. A 1 means something changed on `main`, so halt.
   - The dist witnesses.
   - The smokes: `init --host codex` and `--host all`, then `verify-install`; both exit 0.

## Cross-story risks

- **Rule 2 across M6.** The no-flag install changes by exactly:
  - three sanctioned deltas: the `SKILL.md` pointers, `launch_wave.mjs` and `cleargate-protocol.md`;
  - three sanctioned additions: `dispatch-serial.md`, `dispatch_serial.mjs` and `lib/flashcard-gate.mjs`;
  - -11's dynamic enforcement row.

  `.claude/settings.json`, hooks, agents, `CLAUDE.md` and `.mcp.json` stay byte-identical. -13 changes no installed byte. The -09 e2e's census (skills = 3 `SKILL.md`, top level, settings triples) is unaffected by `dispatch-serial.md`. Keep `/private/tmp/cg-s41-m2-init-baseline` until RS-CLOSE (M5).
- **-12 rewrites -11-owned contracts**:
  - portable-skills scenario 6;
  - the `codex.live.skills` verdict and its test;
  - §16 bullet 10.

  QA-Red names each as a deliberate contract change. A Developer who leaves -11's "absent" assertion in place makes pass B unmergeable.
- **The outer-main reads (rule 15).** Two sets of tests read the outer main checkout:
  - `wave-execution-barrier.red` and `bug-034` read the outer main `launch_wave.mjs`;
  - `canonical-live-parity` 5b reads the outer main knowledge docs.

  Pre-merge they grade the old files; the outer-first merge order makes them green. Scenario 1 and scenarios 26–28 (`META_ROOT`) are the pre-merge witnesses.
- **Live drift window.** Between -12's BD and RS-5, BUG-085's doctor warns about `.claude/skills/sprint-execution/**`. RS-5 immediately follows BD.
- **Artifact-input invalidation.** -12 and -13 change `src/**` and `scaffold/**`, so every evidence file goes stale until re-run: RS-5 for -09/-10, and RS-CLOSE for all three.
  - Step L must be the **last** thing before -13's DevOps: after post-flight, never before it.
  - A post-flight FAIL forces step L again.
- **The live primary may fail for model reasons.**
  - The prompts force the tool choice ("use apply_patch, not a shell command"). The negative controls are INCONCLUSIVE (never PASS) when the model does not attempt the forbidden action.
  - Two prepares at most. Then -13 is BLOCKED and the goal verdict is `partial`. That is the honest result, and it is the explicit story OQ4 decision.
- **The live orchestrator may call `request_user_input`.** The harness declines it and records it. If the model then stops, the loop FAILs transport. That is a real finding about the Codex rendering, and it becomes a follow-up.
- **M5's scripted controls.** M5's controls send a top-level `apply_patch` that the catalog does not list (X1); Codex accepts it. -13's scripts use the code-mode `exec` shape a real model emits. Both fire identical hook payloads (X4), so the two harnesses stay comparable.
- **Sandbox `.git` (X8) is a product gap, not a harness artefact.** A Codex user on the default sandbox cannot run the loop's git steps. -13 discloses the posture and files the CR. Do not silently add `writable_roots` to `-10`'s `injectCodexConfig` inside M6: that is an installed-byte change to `.codex/config.toml` and needs its own item.
- **Role-shell PATH (X9)** is a clean-room concern. In a user's repo, their login PATH is their own. The `.zprofile` fix lives only in `<C>/home`.
- **Dispatch identity under D+.** The S4 probe uses `STORY=001-99`, so its `codex-identity` line cannot be counted as a loop dispatch. **[verify after -10: the sentinel's work-item extraction from `tool_input.message`]**
- **Stale reports.** The five 2026-09-12 `STORY-058-12-*` reports must move before wave16's QA-Red. Otherwise new reports overwrite recorded history, and a TPV reads the wrong file.
- **The CG_TEST executor is not a starting point.** `refs/cgtest/STORY-058-12` does not exist in the cli repo (only BUG-067 and BUG-089 were fetched), and its design (an app-server wave executor) contradicts the approved story (§1.3: "Any Codex-side wave/parallel scheduler" is out of scope). No code is ported (rule 13).

It takes about 2 minutes, and nothing can substitute for it. The same ask covers the primary run's use of the owner's Codex quota: six or more agents on `gpt-5.6-terra`, 20–90 minutes of wall-clock. ## Appendix — pinned texts

### dispatch-serial.md (canonical `scaffold/hosts/claude/.claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md`)

```markdown
# Serial Dispatch Contract

This contract is the dispatch path for a host whose adapter declares `dispatchMode: serial` — a host with no Workflow primitive. It replaces the playbook's wave launch (§C.0.1) and nothing else: §22 (autonomy and the five true-blocker kinds), the per-story steps §C.1–§C.9, every role boundary, and every rule in `cleargate-enforcement.md` apply unchanged. A host that declares `dispatchMode: wave-parallel` never uses this file.

## 1. When this contract applies

- The installed playbook for this host names this file in §2 and §C.0.
- `dispatchMode` is fixed when ClearGate is installed. It is never chosen per session, and it is never a fallback for a host that has a Workflow primitive but cannot reach it — that host halts (§C.0).

## 2. The serial loop

Run the waves in `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/plans/waves.json` order and, inside a wave, its stories in `stories` order. Exactly one story is in flight at any time, and inside a story exactly one role dispatch is in flight at any time.

1. Ask for the next action:
   `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs next --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id>`
   It prints one JSON object. Act on its `action`:
   - `process-flashcards` (exit 4) — process every listed card exactly as §C.9 says (approve or reject, then write its `.processed-<hash>` marker), then ask again.
   - `dispatch` (exit 0) — continue with step 2 for the named `storyId`.
   - `halt` (exit 3) — a recorded verdict for this story is not GREEN. Stop and surface its blocker to the human in this turn.
   - `wave-complete` (exit 0) — go to the next wave.
2. Create the story's worktree with exactly the printed `worktreeCommand` — `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-X -b story/STORY-X sprint/S-NN` — unless `worktreeExists` is `true`.
3. Run the story through §C.1–§C.7. Each role works only inside `.worktrees/STORY-X`, plus its report under `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/reports/`. Dispatch one role, wait for its final report, then decide the next dispatch. Never start a second role while one is still running.
4. Write the story's verdict (§5) to a file and record it:
   `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs record --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id> --verdict <file>`
   Exit 0: the verdict is GREEN — go back to step 1. Exit 3: the verdict is not GREEN — halt now, in this turn, and dispatch nothing else. Exit 1: the verdict is malformed — fix the verdict, never the validator.

## 3. Invariants reproduced

| Invariant | How the serial path keeps it |
|---|---|
| One ClearGate-managed worktree per story, cut from the sprint branch | `next` prints the command the wave path builds (`worktreeAddCommand` in `launch_wave.mjs`); worktrees are created one at a time |
| Flashcard write-gate per story (`cleargate-enforcement.md` §4.3) | `next` refuses to name the next story until every flagged card has a `.processed-<hash>` marker; the host's dispatch hook enforces the same gate (§4.6) at every role dispatch |
| Exactly-once dispatch attribution | the host's own dispatch hook chain attributes each role dispatch once (a dispatch marker or sentinel, claimed when the subagent stops); never write attribution by hand |
| Single-writer serial merge | DevOps merges each GREEN story to `sprint/S-NN` (§C.7) before the next story starts |
| True-blocker halt (§22) | a non-GREEN verdict halts the loop synchronously in the orchestrator's own turn |
| Idempotent resume | `next --resume` re-dispatches only the blocked story, reuses its worktree, and never re-dispatches a GREEN story |

## 4. Deliberately omitted

These three mechanisms exist only to work around a parallel wave; copying them would weaken the serial path.

| Mechanism (wave path) | Why the wave path has it | Why this contract omits it |
|---|---|---|
| `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE=1` for the whole wave | a parallel dispatch cannot give each segment its own environment, so the write-gate is relocated to the barrier | one story runs at a time, so the original per-story gate (§4.3) applies unmodified; setting the variable would silently widen the flashcard blind spot. Never set it during a serial run. |
| `RUN_ID`-keyed barrier ledger rows | under a Workflow run the dispatch hook never fires and the subagent stop event carries the orchestrator's session | serial dispatches go through the host's own dispatch hook chain; where the host exposes no verifiable usage, no usage row is written at all |
| Verdict returned instead of halting | a parallel run cannot stop for a human mid-run | the serial loop halts in its own turn, which is strictly stronger |

## 5. Verdict

Every story ends with one verdict object — the protocol §23.1 envelope, validated with `validateVerdicts(verdicts, { mode: 'serial' })`:

- `verdict` (`GREEN`, `ESCALATED` or `BLOCKED`), `storyId`, `runId` (the `runId` that `next` printed), `counters` (`qa_bounces`, `arch_bounces`, `breaker_hits`) and `flashcards_flagged` (the union of the dev and QA reports' cards).
- `devSha`, `qaSha`: required on GREEN; `null` on a non-GREEN verdict when that commit does not exist. Never invent a SHA.
- `tokens`: the measured object when the host reports verifiable usage, otherwise exactly `{ "metering": "unavailable" }`. Never zeros.
- `blocker` (`type`, `message`): required on a non-GREEN verdict; `type` is one of the five §22 kinds.

## 6. Resume after a blocker

After the human resolves a blocker, run `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs next --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id> --resume`. It names the blocked story again (`worktreeExists: true`). Run it from the step the blocker interrupted, record its new verdict, and continue with step 1. GREEN stories are never re-dispatched.
```

### Codex rendering replacements (`scripts/codex-sprint-execution.mjs`)

**R5** — host note (A5):
```
> **Host: Codex — `dispatchMode: serial`.** This file is generated from ClearGate's canonical sprint-execution playbook for hosts without a Workflow primitive. §2 and §C.0 point at `dispatch-serial.md` (this directory): read it before §C. Where the rest of this playbook names a Claude Code mechanism, use the Codex one:
>
> | Playbook text | Under Codex |
> |---|---|
> | spawn via the `Agent` tool with `subagent_type=<role>` | `spawn_agent` with `agent_type: "<role>"`, `fork_turns: "none"` and the complete dispatch brief as `message`; then call `wait_agent` until that agent's final answer arrives. `spawn_agent` returns immediately, so never spawn the next role before the previous one's final answer. |
> | `.claude/agents/<role>.md` | `.codex/agents/<role>.toml` |
> | `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` | `.agents/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` |
> | `.claude/hooks/<hook>.sh` | `.codex/hooks/<hook>.sh` |
> | `write_dispatch.sh` fallback blocks, `PreToolUse:Task` | skip the fallback: the `Agent\|.*spawn_agent` dispatch hook and the SubagentStop hook attribute every dispatch; metering is unavailable (`cleargate-enforcement.md` §16) |
> | `AskUserQuestion` | `request_user_input`, under the same §22 rule: true blockers only |
> | restart Claude Code | start a new Codex session |
```

**R1** (A1):
```
There is one and only one way a sprint executes on this host, and it has no toggle. Every rule in `cleargate-enforcement.md` is **always enforced** (`CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` is the sole break-glass, and it only softens gate *strength*, never the execution path). This host has no Workflow primitive, so ClearGate installed it with `dispatchMode: serial`: every sprint runs its Architect-produced `waves.json` one story at a time through the serial dispatch contract in `dispatch-serial.md` (§C.0). There is no parallel wave launch on this host and no other loop.
```

**R2** (A2):
```
### C.0 Execution — the serial dispatch contract (`dispatch-serial.md`)

Run every wave from the Architect's `waves.json` through `dispatch-serial.md`: one story in flight at a time, in wave order, each in its own ClearGate-managed `.worktrees/STORY-X` cut from `sprint/S-NN`; the flashcard gate between every pair of consecutive stories; and a synchronous halt, in your own turn, on any non-GREEN verdict. `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs next` names the next action and `… record` validates and records each story's verdict. The per-story steps §C.1–§C.9 below are the body of each serial step.
```

**R3** (A3):
```
**Serial merge.** Stories run one at a time, so DevOps merges each GREEN `story/STORY-X` to `sprint/S-NN` as soon as that story passes — one worktree at a time, never two concurrently; `sprint/S-NN` is a single-writer axis. A story whose verdict is not GREEN is not merged: record its verdict and halt (`dispatch-serial.md` §2). After the human resolves the blocker, `dispatch_serial.mjs next --resume` re-dispatches only that story.
```

**R4** (A4):
```
The dispatch hook (`pending-task-sentinel.sh`, registered for `Agent|.*spawn_agent`) blocks the next role dispatch until every card has a `.processed-<hash>` marker, and `dispatch_serial.mjs next` will not name the next story until they exist.

**Per-story gate.** On this host the gate runs between every pair of consecutive stories. It is never relocated and never suppressed: do not set `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE` during a sprint (`dispatch-serial.md` §4).
```

### protocol §23.5 (canonical `scaffold/core/.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md`, after §23.4)

```
### 23.5 Serial dispatch contract (`dispatchMode: serial`)

Hosts without a Workflow primitive run every wave one story at a time through `.cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs`, their generated rendering of the sprint-execution playbook, and its `dispatch-serial.md`. §22 applies unchanged. Differences from §23.1–§23.4:

- **Verdict (§23.1).** Same envelope, validated by `validateVerdicts(verdicts, { mode: 'serial' })`. `tokens` is either the measured object or exactly `{ "metering": "unavailable" }`; a non-GREEN verdict may carry `devSha: null` / `qaSha: null` when no commit exists. GREEN still requires both SHAs. The default mode is unchanged.
- **Attribution (§23.2).** There is no RUN_ID-keyed barrier row. The host's own dispatch hook chain attributes each role dispatch; when the host exposes no verifiable usage, nothing is written to the usage ledger — never a zero row.
- **Flashcards (§23.3).** The gate runs between every pair of consecutive stories (`cleargate-enforcement.md` §4.3) and is never suppressed.
- **Merge (§23.3).** DevOps merges each GREEN story as soon as it passes — one worktree at a time.
- **Blockers (§23.4).** A non-GREEN verdict halts the loop synchronously in the orchestrator's own turn; `dispatch_serial.mjs next --resume` re-dispatches only the blocked story after the human resolves it.
```

### enforcement §17 (canonical `scaffold/core/.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-enforcement.md`, appended)

```
## 17. Host Dispatch Modes (source: STORY-058-12)

Each host adapter declares `dispatchMode` (`cleargate-cli/src/hosts/types.ts`): Claude Code `wave-parallel`, Codex `serial`. It is static and read at install time: `.agents/skills/sprint-execution/` — the generated serial rendering of the playbook plus `dispatch-serial.md` — is installed only for a selected host that declares the `.agents` shared root and `dispatchMode: serial`.

Under `dispatchMode: serial`:

1. **Flashcard gate.** `dispatch_serial.mjs next` refuses the next story while any `flashcards_flagged` card lacks its `.processed-<hash>` marker (the §4.6 scan, ported to `.cleargate/scripts/lib/flashcard-gate.mjs` and pinned to the hook by a parity test). The host dispatch hook still enforces §4.6 at every role dispatch. `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE` is never set in a serial run.
2. **Verdicts.** `dispatch_serial.mjs record` validates with `validateVerdicts(…, { mode: 'serial' })` and exits 3 on a non-GREEN verdict; the orchestrator halts in that turn.
3. **Worktrees.** The same command the wave path builds (`worktreeAddCommand`), one story at a time.
4. **Usage.** No usage-ledger row for `metering: unavailable`; `dispatch_serial.mjs` never writes `token-ledger.jsonl`.
```

### E0. GOAL_RELATION

GOAL_RELATION: advances.

-12 gives the five-agent loop a dispatch path under a second host. Codex receives a static `dispatchMode: serial` install: a generated serial rendering of the playbook, `dispatch-serial.md`, and `dispatch_serial.mjs`. The Claude path stays byte-identical apart from two sanctioned pointer paragraphs. That is the precondition for Goal Acceptance Check condition 5 (proved by STORY-058-13). RS-5 then re-witnesses conditions 2–4 on the result.

**E3.2 The dangling pointer (CR-124 post-flight): resolved in the rendering.**
- §A.4 `:207` ends `§C.0.2 runs such a plan.` That line is outside every range, while A2 (which the render replaces with R2) removes `§C.0.1` and `§C.0.2`. `dispatch-serial.md` (M6 appendix plus the RP-A16 paragraph) also cites `§C.0.1` and `§C.0.2`. Its only operative reader is the serial orchestrator, whose rendered playbook has neither section.
- **Rejected: keeping §C.0.2 in the render** (narrowing A2 to end before `#### C.0.2`). Its rule 3 ("every story whose base exists builds at once … `launchWave({…, bases})` … within the plan's build lanes") contradicts the serial contract.
- **Decision (binding):**
  1. **A6** is a sixth anchored replacement. In the single line starting `**Contract-first output (CR-124).**` (unique, `:207`), the exact sentence `§C.0.2 runs such a plan.` (unique in that line) is replaced with R6. A missing line or a missing sentence throws `codex-sprint-execution: anchor not found: A6`.
  2. `CODEX_SKILL_BANNED` gains `C.0.1` and `C.0.2` (11 tokens). A future pointer from outside A2 into a replaced subsection then fails the build instead of dangling.
  3. R2 gains one sentence for `schema_version: 2` plans (E6.9).
  4. `dispatch-serial.md` drops both `§C.0.x` pointers (intro and the RP-A16 paragraph), and test 25 forbids `C.0.1` and `C.0.2` in it. The whole Codex sprint-execution surface then names no §C.0 subsection.
- **Measured by scratch simulation on C2 bytes.** It inserts P1/P2, then applies A5, A1, A2, A3, A4 and A6 with R1–R6. Results:
  - 0 survivors of the 11 tokens; the output has 811 lines; P1 and P2 do not survive.
  - The two-insertion round trip is exact.
  - CR-124's T3b (`contract-first-dag-text.red.node.test.ts:201-214`) still finds each of the 7 anchors exactly once after P1/P2, because P1 and P2 quote no anchor text.
  - R1–R6 carry no banned token and no exec-mode vocabulary. R1 intentionally starts with the A1 anchor prefix; that is harmless, because the renderer never re-renders its own output.

The array closes with `]);` at `:752`. `SANCTIONED_BASELINE_ADDITIONS` holds -11's `lib/harness.sh` (`:803-813`), then `]);` at `:814`. This confirms the dispatch's "-10 x3, -11 x4, CR-124 x6, shared x1".

**Reused rows. -12 adds no row for these rels:**
- CR-124's SKILL.md row (`:689-703`) refuses only `c5affd1b…`.
- CR-124's launch_wave.mjs row (`:706-718`) refuses only `aa04cd43…`.
- The shared enforcement row refuses only `4c3e8083…`.

All three read pin-substituted canonical, so they accept -12's bytes. A second row for any of these rels fails its baseline assert (one row per rel). The comment in CR-124's SKILL.md row ("each item keeps its own row") is stale, but **do not edit it**: touching CR-124's row turns a mechanical integration into a QA-Red conflict.

**-12's rows.** They append at the two tails.

(a) The protocol row goes after `:751`, before `]);` (refuse-baseline pattern):
```ts
  {
    item: 'STORY-058-12',
    rel: '.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md',
    scenarios: 'all',
    // Canonical bytes at C2 8c8c5ff (= cli main 984e8d5 = 35580b0); no pin placeholder.
    baselineHash: () => 'be92d45a217ca4a510c0589ec467933599302494e9cb1114004f1b19d35b8e2b',
    currentHash: () => {
      const h = currentPinSubstitutedHash('.cleargate/knowledge/cleargate-protocol.md');
      return h === 'be92d45a217ca4a510c0589ec467933599302494e9cb1114004f1b19d35b8e2b'
        ? '<STORY-058-12 cleargate-protocol.md delta not landed: canonical still ' + h + '>'
        : h;
    },
  },
```

(b) Three additions go after `:813`, before `]);`. The same block repeats for `.cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs` and `.cleargate/scripts/lib/flashcard-gate.mjs`:
```ts
  {
    item: 'STORY-058-12',
    rel: '.claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md',
    scenarios: 'all',
    mode: 0o644,
    currentHash: () => currentPinSubstitutedHash('.claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md'),
    stdoutLine: (scenario) =>
      scenario === 'reinit'
        ? '[cleargate init] Skipped (exists) .claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md\n'
        : '[cleargate init] Created .claude/skills/sprint-execution/dispatch-serial.md\n',
  },
```

**Why 0o644.** All three files are committed `100644`, have no `.sh` suffix, and `copyPayload`'s `needsExec` is "source has an exec bit or ends in `.sh`" (`src/init/copy-payload.ts:178-180`). Invoke `dispatch_serial.mjs` as `node <file>`, and never commit it `100755`.

**The stdoutLine pattern** follows `copyPayload`'s branches (`:182-230`): a new file prints `Created`, and the `reinit` second pass finds identical bytes and prints `Skipped (exists)`. `.cleargate/scripts/**` being `FIRST_INSTALL_ONLY` changes nothing for a file that does not yet exist. QA-Red still measures each scenario on a conformant build before sealing.

**E6.4 AD-M6-4 additions (`dispatch_serial.mjs`).**
- Before any use, `next` and `record` reject with exit 2 (usage, nothing on stdout) when any of these holds:
  - a wave id does not match `^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$` or is `.` or `..`;
  - `plans/waves.json` has a `schema_version` other than absent or `2`;
  - the wave is missing, or its `stories` is not an array;
  - any `wave.stories` entry fails `extractWorkItemIds(id)` deep-equal `[id]`.
- The wave is resolved as `waves[].wave === <id>` and uses `wave.stories`. A `schema_version: 2` top-level `stories` map, `contracts` and every `base` are **ignored**, so each worktree command ends in `sprintBranchFor(sprintId)`.
- The only env var the module reads is `ORCHESTRATOR_PROJECT_DIR` (repo-root fallback). It reads neither `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE` nor `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY`: the driver gate is not a hook, and the host's dispatch hook keeps honouring the advisory break-glass per dispatch.
- The `next` answer `process-flashcards` carries `unparseable: string[]` (FU-7), and exits 4.
- Upstream enforcement for `schema_version: 2` plans is **not** added to `next`. Running the derived `waves` in order, with a serial merge before the next story, already guarantees it. `integrationReady` alone would be weaker (flashcard `#sdr #waves`).

**E6.5 AD-M6-5 (flashcard-gate port) at C2.**
- **Port target:** the post-(-11) sentinel. Glob `:113-116`, JSON `:118-156`, parser loop `:161-240`, gate `:242`.
- **API (FU-7):**
  - `cardHash(card)`;
  - `flaggedCards(text) → { cards: string[], unterminated: boolean }`;
  - `reportFiles(sprintDir)` — regular files (following symlinks) matching `*-dev.md`/`*-qa.md` directly in `sprintDir` and in `sprintDir/reports`; names starting with `.` are excluded, as the bash `*` glob excludes them;
  - `pendingFlashcards({ sprintDir }) → { ok, unprocessed: [{card,hash,report}], unparseable: string[] }`, deduped by hash. `ok` is false on any unprocessed card **or** any unparseable file.
- **Porting rule (new).** Bash ERE `.` and `[[:space:]]` both match `\r`, but JS `.` does not. Port:
  - every `.` as `[\s\S]`;
  - `[[:space:]]` as `[ \t\n\v\f\r]`;
  - the JSON string as `"((?:[^"\\]|\\[\s\S])*)"`;
  - `^[a-zA-Z_]` as ASCII.

  Otherwise JS drops the `\r` from CRLF list cards (a different hash) and misses a CRLF JSON open (`\[(.*)$`). Parity fixtures F6 and F13 kill that mutant.
- **Per-line check order** is exactly the hook's: JSON mode → inline `[]` → JSON open → YAML key → md heading → in-block stop → list item.
- Other semantics: unescape `\"` and then `\\`; list strip in the order leading `"`, trailing `"`, leading `'`, trailing `'`; skip empty cards; a trailing `''` split segment is a no-op.

**E6.6 AD-M6-6 (verdict mode). Messages pinned.**
- `options` undefined, null, or with `mode` undefined → `'wave'`. An unknown mode → `TypeError("validateVerdicts: unknown mode '<m>'")`, thrown before iterating.
- In serial mode:

  | Verdict shape | Result |
  |---|---|
  | `tokens` has an own `metering` key | must be exactly `{ metering: 'unavailable' }`, else `'tokens' must be the measured object or exactly { metering: 'unavailable' }` |
  | `tokens` has no `metering` key | the default measured checks and messages |
  | non-GREEN with `devSha`/`qaSha` `null` | accepted |
  | non-GREEN with the key absent or non-string non-null | `'devSha' must be a non-empty string, or null on a non-GREEN serial verdict` (and the `qaSha` analog) |
  | GREEN | the default scalar check, so `null` gives `missing or empty required field 'devSha'` |

- Every message is still wrapped as `Malformed segment verdict for <id>: <reason>.`.
- The header schema block (`:26-39`) documents the serial deltas.
- Nothing outside the header block, `verdictMalformationReason`/`validateVerdicts` (`:145-248`) and the usage string changes.

````markdown
# Serial Dispatch Contract

This contract is the dispatch path for a host whose adapter declares `dispatchMode: serial` — a host with no Workflow primitive. It replaces the playbook's wave launch and nothing else: §22 (autonomy and the five true-blocker kinds), the per-story steps §C.1–§C.9, every role boundary, and every rule in `cleargate-enforcement.md` apply unchanged. A host that declares `dispatchMode: wave-parallel` never uses this file.

## 1. When this contract applies

- The installed playbook for this host names this file in §2 and §C.0.
- `dispatchMode` is fixed when ClearGate is installed. It is never chosen per session, and it is never a fallback for a host that has a Workflow primitive but cannot reach it — that host halts (§C.0).

## 2. The serial loop

Run the waves in `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/plans/waves.json` order and, inside a wave, its stories in `stories` order. Exactly one story is in flight at any time, and inside a story exactly one role dispatch is in flight at any time.

**Contract-first plans.** A `waves.json` with `schema_version: 2` adds contracts and per-story `base` and `upstream`. A serial host ignores `contracts[]` and `base`: it runs the derived `waves` exactly as above, and it never starts a story before every story in its `upstream` has merged, so each story is cut from `sprint/S-NN` with its upstream already merged into it — there is no contract branch to create and no separate integration step.

1. Ask for the next action:
   `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs next --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id>`
   It prints one JSON object. Act on its `action`:
   - `process-flashcards` (exit 4) — process every listed card exactly as §C.9 says (approve or reject, then write its `.processed-<hash>` marker), rewrite every listed `unparseable` report's `flashcards_flagged` block as a `- "card"` list, then ask again.
   - `dispatch` (exit 0) — continue with step 2 for the named `storyId`.
   - `halt` (exit 3) — a recorded verdict for this story is not GREEN. Stop and surface its blocker to the human in this turn.
   - `wave-complete` (exit 0) — go to the next wave.
2. Create the story's worktree with exactly the printed `worktreeCommand` — `git worktree add .worktrees/STORY-X -b story/STORY-X sprint/S-NN` — unless `worktreeExists` is `true`.
3. Run the story through §C.1–§C.7. Each role works only inside `.worktrees/STORY-X`, plus its report under `.cleargate/sprint-runs/<sprint-id>/reports/`. Dispatch one role, wait for its final report, then decide the next dispatch. Never start a second role while one is still running.
4. Write the story's verdict (§5) to a file and record it:
   `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs record --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id> --verdict <file>`
   Exit 0: the verdict is GREEN — go back to step 1. Exit 3: the verdict is not GREEN — halt now, in this turn, and dispatch nothing else. Exit 1: the verdict is malformed — fix the verdict, never the validator.

## 3. Invariants reproduced

| Invariant | How the serial path keeps it |
|---|---|
| One ClearGate-managed worktree per story, cut from the sprint branch | `next` prints the command the wave path builds (`worktreeAddCommand` in `launch_wave.mjs`); worktrees are created one at a time |
| Flashcard write-gate per story (`cleargate-enforcement.md` §4.3) | `next` refuses to name the next story until every flagged card has a `.processed-<hash>` marker and no report's `flashcards_flagged` block is unparseable; the host's dispatch hook enforces the same gate (§4.6) at every role dispatch |
| Exactly-once dispatch attribution | the host's own dispatch hook chain attributes each role dispatch once (a dispatch marker or sentinel, claimed when the subagent stops); never write attribution by hand |
| Single-writer serial merge | DevOps merges each GREEN story to `sprint/S-NN` (§C.7) before the next story starts |
| True-blocker halt (§22) | a non-GREEN verdict halts the loop synchronously in the orchestrator's own turn |
| Idempotent resume | `next --resume` re-dispatches only the blocked story, reuses its worktree, and never re-dispatches a GREEN story |

## 4. Deliberately omitted

These three mechanisms exist only to work around a parallel wave; copying them would weaken the serial path.

| Mechanism (wave path) | Why the wave path has it | Why this contract omits it |
|---|---|---|
| `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE=1` for the whole wave | a parallel dispatch cannot give each segment its own environment, so the write-gate is relocated to the barrier | one story runs at a time, so the original per-story gate (§4.3) applies unmodified; setting the variable would silently widen the flashcard blind spot. Never set it during a serial run. |
| `RUN_ID`-keyed barrier ledger rows | under a Workflow run the dispatch hook never fires and the subagent stop event carries the orchestrator's session | serial dispatches go through the host's own dispatch hook chain; where the host exposes no verifiable usage, no usage row is written at all |
| Verdict returned instead of halting | a parallel run cannot stop for a human mid-run | the serial loop halts in its own turn, which is strictly stronger |

## 5. Verdict

Every story ends with one verdict object — the protocol §23.1 envelope, validated with `validateVerdicts(verdicts, { mode: 'serial' })`:

- `verdict` (`GREEN`, `ESCALATED` or `BLOCKED`), `storyId`, `runId` (the `runId` that `next` printed), `counters` (`qa_bounces`, `arch_bounces`, `breaker_hits`) and `flashcards_flagged` (the union of the dev and QA reports' cards).
- `devSha`, `qaSha`: required on GREEN; `null` on a non-GREEN verdict when that commit does not exist. Never invent a SHA.
- `tokens`: the measured object when the host reports verifiable usage, otherwise exactly `{ "metering": "unavailable" }`. Never zeros.
- `blocker` (`type`, `message`): required on a non-GREEN verdict; `type` is one of the five §22 kinds.

## 6. Resume after a blocker

After the human resolves a blocker, run `node .cleargate/scripts/dispatch_serial.mjs next --sprint <sprint-id> --wave <wave-id> --resume`. It names the blocked story again (`worktreeExists: true`). Run it from the step the blocker interrupted, record its new verdict, and continue with step 1. GREEN stories are never re-dispatched.
````

2. The dangling `§C.0.2` pointer is fixed by A6/R6, by banning `C.0.x` in the render, and by `dispatch-serial.md` dropping its `§C.0.x` pointers. §C.0.2 is not kept in the render.

4. `dispatch_serial.mjs next` ignores `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY` and adds no upstream check (E6.4).
5. The enforcement `:103` rewording and the `collision_surface.sh:40` comment stay follow-ups outside -12.
6. CR-124's stale comment in the init-fixture SKILL.md row is not edited.

## Amendments from TPV (STORY-058-12, 2026-09-26; role: architect; append-only)

Source: `reports/STORY-058-12-tpv.md`. Binding for STORY-058-12. Where these disagree with E0–E12 or anything above, these win. They change no Gherkin, no scope, no file list and no test count.

**TPV-A1: Re-seal before any Developer dispatch.**
- `reports/STORY-058-12-tpv-reseal.patch` (sha256 `ed47ca875b6a2c00ff5bb4811f9e821b0c608820e21bcb592bdd47a59c9f76ef`) replaces 8 sealed test files on cli `story/STORY-058-12` @ `e320ec4`. It touches test files only.
- Counts are unchanged: default tier +52, integration tier +1; at `e320ec4` the reds stay 62 and the anchors stay 2 (`dispatch-verdict-modes` Test 1, `skill-md-serial-pointers` Test 21). E8's per-file table stands.
- Six sealed assertions fail on a plan-conformant build and are fixed by the re-seal:
  1. `dispatch-serial` Test 5 yields two microtask ticks; three awaited seams (gate, worktreeExists, createWorktree) precede `dispatchStory`, so no conformant build reaches it. The re-seal yields one macrotask (`setImmediate`).
  2. `dispatch-serial` Test 7 asserted that the BLOCKED verdict is **not** recorded. AD-M6-4 records every dispatched verdict, then halts. The re-seal asserts `record(STORY-001-02)` is the last seam call.
  3. `skill-md-serial-pointers` Test 20 read the P2 blank line at fixture index +2; after P1 the anchor itself sits at +2. The re-seal reads +3/+4.
  4. `dispatch-mode` Test 30's claude-like fake declared `sharedRoots ['.agents']`, so `f(['claude'])` admits the flashcard skill under today's rule. The re-seal gives it `ownedRoots ['.claude']`, `sharedRoots []`.
  5. and 6. `init-codex-sprint-execution` Tests 35/36 composed a payload without `hosts/claude` (`[]` / `['codex']`), so no `.claude/**` could install. The re-seal uses `['claude']` / `['claude','codex']`.

**TPV-A2: Contract points the re-seal now pins.** These restate AD-M6-4/-5/-6, E6.2–E6.6; they add no behavior.
1. `validateVerdicts` serial mode: an absent `qaSha` on a non-GREEN verdict is rejected with `'qaSha' must be a non-empty string, or null on a non-GREEN serial verdict` (the E6.6 analog).
2. `dispatchSerial` validates and records every dispatched verdict, GREEN or not; a halted run's last seam call is `recordVerdict(<storyId>)`.
3. `dispatch_serial.mjs`:
   - `next` exits 4 on a pending card even with `CLEARGATE_ADVISORY=1` and `SKIP_FLASHCARD_GATE=1` in its env (E6.4: the driver and `lib/flashcard-gate.mjs` read neither).
   - `record` rejects a well-formed verdict whose `storyId` is not in `wave.stories`: exit 1, stderr names the storyId, nothing appended.
   - The wave-id check runs before the wave lookup: `--wave ../x` exits 2 even when `waves.json` declares a wave `../x`.
   - The main guard compares realpaths: the CLI invoked through a symlinked directory still runs (no subcommand → exit 2, not a silent 0).
4. Frozen exports are pinned by direct import: `SERIAL_EXIT`, `sprintBranchFor`, `serialLogPath`, `readVerdictLog`, `nextAction`; `flaggedCards(text) → { cards, unterminated }`; `reportFiles(sprintDir)` excludes dot-files and directories.
5. `renderCodexSprintExecution`:
   - Test 31 pins `CODEX_SKILL_BANNED` (the 11 E6.2 tokens, in order) and `CODEX_SKILL_REPLACEMENTS` (`R1`–`R6`, M6 appendix and E6.9 texts) by value, requires each R text verbatim in the output, and checks that every canonical line outside A1–A4 appears in order, with the A6 line carrying R6.
   - Test 32 deletes each of A1, A2, A4, A5, A6 (line) and the A6 sentence alone; each throws `anchor not found: A<n>`.
   - Consequence: resolve all nine anchor lines (prefix matches, including the three range ends) before any splice or banned-token check. Deleting the A1 line leaves P1's `/workflows` in place, so a banned-first order reports the wrong error. A2's end anchor must be a prefix match: test 32 appends ` (launch_wave)` to the `### C.1 Pre-execution check` line and expects `banned token survived: launch_wave`.
   - A5 splice (literal reading of "preceded and followed by one blank line"): insert one empty line and R5's 11 lines directly after the `# Sprint Execution — The Playbook` line; the canonical blank line after the heading closes R5. On C2 + P1/P2 the render is 824 lines, sha256 `ec54c4013fd4db47de5170c7f2095e318fa523739502d1968983e0456e2740b3`. E3.2's "811 lines" is superseded.

**TPV-A3: Pass-A target hashes (fully determined by the pinned texts, measured on C2 bytes and unchanged on the integrated tree).**
- canonical `SKILL.md` `cecf1700864c631e18bb8efbd31ccc751e200dbf2fd52a2684b87c8782cf3bc0` (`git diff --numstat` 4/0)

**TPV-A4: Named build-phase reds, additions to E8.** Each was measured identical on `e320ec4` and on the conformant build, and each clears at integration:
- `close-sprint-step-2-6c` S5, `close-sprint-step-7-4` S3, `template-claude-md` (3 template cases), `wiki-ingest-sprint-report` S14: BUG-090 two-repo parity (C2's canonical predates BUG-090; `<ow>` carries BUG-090's live twins).

- `addHost` builds its own copy filter from the added adapter's `ownedRoots() ∪ sharedRoots()` (`src/hosts/lifecycle.ts:293-294`), not from `payloadFilter`. The serial-surface rule therefore reaches `init`, the snapshot, carry-forward, host removal (`lifecycle.ts:117`), doctor and upgrade, but not `cleargate host add`. Measured: with `codexAdapter.dispatchMode` flipped to `wave-parallel`, `lifecycle-codex` stays 6/6.

- -12 does not change `lifecycle.ts`; the E7 allow-list stands. File a follow-up under EPIC-058 at close, as a prerequisite of the first `wave-parallel` host that declares `.agents` (the deferred Copilot adapter): apply the serial-surface rule inside `addHost`'s include and add a fixture-registry `host add` case.

### F3. Role and agent listings (M5-A55)

- **No -13 check parses a Codex role listing.** `codex.live.roles` is not in `ENFORCEMENT_IDS` (M6:737, M5-A55 "-12 and -13 coupling"). `codex.roles` (verify-install) parses TOML files, never a listing.
- `codex.loop.dispatches` takes `agent_type` from three raw sources:
  - `JSON.parse(function_call.arguments).agent_type` for spawns;
  - the ` codex-identity ` log line via the token regex `(?:^| )agent_type=(\S+)(?: |$)`;
  - rollout `session_meta` via `JSON.parse` of each rollout line.
  Never a regex over `JSON.stringify` of any object.
- **Mock route markers match over the raw request body text** (`bodyText`, `mock-responses.mjs:47`), never over a re-serialized object. Every marker contains no `"`, `\` or control character, so its JSON encoding equals itself and a substring match is exact.
- **No marker may be a substring of another.** Test 20a asserts this. It is why S3/S4 use `CG-CONTROL-SPAWN-DENY` / `CG-CONTROL-SPAWN-ALLOW`, not M6's shared `CG-CONTROL-DISPATCH`.
- **Any -13 fixture that models the `spawn_agent` `agent_type` schema** (none is planned) uses the measured one-per-line layout: a header, `Available roles:`, then per role `<name>: {`, the description, `}`. Never a comma list.

- **Split-view availability.** A split-dependent check whose `ctx.splitViewRoot` is not a string, or whose `<split>/hosts/codex` (or `<split>/core/.agents/skills` for `codex.skills`) is not a directory, returns `fail` with the single detail `split view unavailable: <String(ctx.splitViewRoot)>`. It never throws and never passes. The split-dependent ids are hooks-wiring, instruction-block (content compare only) and roles; skills uses the core root.

**F6.3 Pass B — harness module deltas (additive; every sealed -10/-11 test stays green).**
- **`mock-responses.mjs`** gains options `routes`, `usage` and a returned `served`/`unrouted`, as M6:675-683.
  - Precedence: `routes` given → route by the first marker found in `bodyText`. Otherwise the existing `respond`, otherwise `script`.
  - Passing both `routes` and `respond` throws `TypeError('startMockResponses: routes and respond are mutually exclusive')`.
  - `usage(i)` fills `response.completed.response.usage`. The default stays the `USAGE` constant.
  - `url` stays the `/v1` base (Test 42 unchanged).
- **`codex-appserver.mjs`**:
  - `runTurn` gains an optional `threadParams` object, spread into `thread/start` after the three existing keys (default `{}`, so today's params are byte-identical).
  - New `runLoopTurn(client, { cwd, prompt, model, config, idleMs = 15000, timeoutMs })` = `runTurn(client, { cwd, prompt, timeoutMs, threadParams: { model, config }, settle })`. `settle` holds when every thread's last `thread/status/changed` is idle and no notification arrived for `idleMs`. It catches runTurn's timeout/exit rejection and resolves `{ timedOut: true, reason, notifications: client.notifications.slice(start) }`; it never rejects.
  - `client.configWrites` (initialized 0) is incremented inside `request()` when `method === 'config/batchWrite'`.
  - New `listProjectHooks(client, roomRealpath)` = `flattenHooksList(await client.request('hooks/list', { cwds: [roomRealpath] }))`.
